# Development Roadmap - USB4 and Thunderbolt 3, the Connection Manager Driver

The fourth roadmap file, opened on 2026-10-14 at the project owner's
request, the day Phase 38 closed on the `2.4.0.0` cut. It carries the
connection-manager work: a driver beside `xhci98.sys` that is a client of
an Intel Thunderbolt controller's firmware connection manager first, and
a USB4 software connection manager after it, so that the PCIe devices
behind a Thunderbolt or USB4 tunnel - a dock's xHCI controller, an NVMe
enclosure - reach the targets this project serves. The three earlier files
are unchanged by it: [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md) keeps the conventions
and Phases 0-16, [`roadmap-miniport-updates.md`](roadmap-miniport-updates.md)
Phases 17-24, and [`roadmap-hcd.md`](roadmap-hcd.md) the host controller
driver's Phases 25 to 38. Every phase from 39 on, USB ones included, is
in this file, and its status line below is the project's current state. Every convention of theirs holds here:
the batching convention and the task-id rules, "observed on both" as
`AGENTS.md` qualifies it, phases numbered when the owner opens them, a
phase's closing task named `<phase>.final`, and the rule that no phase
advances past an unobserved checkpoint.

**Status: no phase is open. Phase 49 is closed on the `3.5.0.0` cut of 2026-11-14, on branch `3.5.0.0` (opened by the owner on 2026-11-10 from the `3.4.3.0` release, going through every planned item; the decisions table, "Phase 49"; 49.final closed by the owner on 2026-11-14), its items left in "Planned - Left by `3.5.0.0`" (the decisions table, "Phase 49 close"). Phase 48 is closed on the `3.4.3.0` cut of 2026-11-10, on branch `3.4.3.0` (opened by the owner on 2026-11-09 from the `3.4.2.0` release, going through every planned item one by one; the decisions table, "Phase 48"), its items left in "Planned - Left by `3.4.3.0`" (the decisions table, "Phase 48 close"). This line is the one place that names the open phase; the other roadmap files, `AGENTS.md` and `docs/README.md` point here. Phase 47 is closed on the `3.4.2.0` cut of 2026-11-08 (opened by the owner on 2026-11-06: the fixes of the whole-repository audit of 2026-11-06 and the five queued `3.4.x` follow-ups, FREENOTE.1, PROGMIN.1, SHUTNOW.1, ISOREC.1 and TRTSC.1; the decisions table, "Phase 47"), its items left in "Planned - Left by `3.4.2.0`"; Phase 46 is closed on the `3.4.1.0` cut of 2026-11-05 (a quick release; `3.4.3.0` until the owner renamed it on 2026-11-01), its items left in "Planned - Left by `3.4.1.0`"; Phase 45 is closed on the `3.4.0.0` cut of 2026-11-01, its bench left in "Planned - Left by `3.4.0.0`"; Phase 44 is closed on the `3.3.0.0` cut of 2026-10-28, its install legs 44-E.7 read after it, its real-hardware bench moved to "Planned work" on 2026-10-28 and from there into Phase 45 (45-E.1 to 45-E.5) on 2026-10-29; Phase 43 is closed on the `3.2.0.0` cut of 2026-10-24; Phase 42 is closed on the `3.1.0.0` cut of 2026-10-20; Phases 39, 40 and 41 are closed on the `3.0.0.0` cut of 2026-10-17.** Phase 46 was opened on 2026-11-01 by the owner, going through every planned item one by one; the decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks and records what was kept. Phase 45 was opened on 2026-10-29 by the owner from "Planned work", going through every planned item one by one; the decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks and records what was kept. Phase 44 was opened on 2026-10-25 by the owner from "Planned work", going through every planned item one by one; the decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks and records what was dropped, closed or kept. Phase 43 was opened on 2026-10-20 by the owner from "Planned work", going through every planned item one by one; the decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks. Phase 42 was opened on 2026-10-18 by the owner from "Planned work" (the windowed installer, the uninstall as NUSB does it, the Windows 98 SE and ME device install, `XHCISNAP` retired, the safe-removal mark across a stack rebuild, and the connection-manager follow-ups), on branch `3.1.0.0`, and widened the same day after the owner went through every planned item; the decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks and records what was dropped or closed. The history of the first three: Phases 39 and 40 opened 2026-10-15 by the owner. The two
pieces of planned work drawn up on 2026-10-14 with the placeholder ids
`TB3` and `U4CM` are Phase 39 and Phase 40, in the order the `usb4-emu`
handoff gives (its section 2, maintainer 2026-10-14): Phase 39 the
Thunderbolt firmware-connection-manager client with tunnel re-enumeration
and safe eject, because it can be proven on the owner's ThinkPad P14s Gen
1; Phase 40 the USB4 software connection manager; real USB4 devices third,
planned task U4HW.1, once a USB4 host that boots these systems is at hand (in Phase 44 from 2026-10-25: 44.39 and 44-E.6; its bench 45-E.5 from 2026-10-29). The release is
`3.0.0.0` and carries both phases (owner, 2026-10-15, decision D7):
`39.final` closes Phase 39 with its legs and a dry build and cuts nothing;
`40.final` cuts `3.0.0.0`. Design record 21 is Phase 39's plan, its
decisions D1 to D13 taken (D4 on 2026-10-14, the rest on 2026-10-15);
design record 22 is Phase 40's.

## The sources this roadmap rests on

The test bed and the hardware contract are the `usb4-emu` repository's
(the `usb4-emu` checkout beside this repository, branch `main`), which this project
reads and never modifies, and whose QEMU processes it never stops:
`docs/XHCI98-HANDOFF.md` is the entry point and names the rest
(`ICM-PROTOCOL.md`, `PLATFORM-MATRIX.md`, `REENUM-EXPERIMENT.md`,
`WIN98-TEST.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `NVME-DRIVER-EVAL.md`, `tools/usb4cm`,
`scripts/run-win98.sh`). Since 2026-10-15 (owner) Linux's
`drivers/thunderbolt` and FreeBSD's `sys/dev/thunderbolt` are references
for this work (ids, layouts, offsets, constants, sequences and quirks
cited by file and symbol), and since `usb4-emu`'s relicensing to
GPL-2.0-only landed the same day (`3c74189`) their code and `usb4-emu`'s
may be adapted, each adapted file keeping its source's copyright lines
and SPDX tag and gaining a `legal-provenance.md` section 2a entry with the
pinned commit (`AGENTS.md`, "Reference Implementations";
`legal-provenance.md` section 3). The
NVMe driver behind the tunnel is `nvme98`
(the `nvme98` checkout beside this repository, `docs/PLAN.md`), read-only here too;
what it asks of this connection manager is design record 21 section 7.
Every virtual-machine leg in this file runs with this project's own
overlays under the git-ignored `vm\usb4\` and its own QMP and VNC ports,
47900 to 47999 (record 21 section 10.2).

## The decisions this roadmap rests on

Design record 21 section 11 lists the thirteen Phase 39 needed (one
binary or two, the NT filter's install route, polling with no interrupt,
the approval policy, the absent controller, the `nvme98` channel, the
version and cut, the names, the other NHI ids, safe eject, the master
switch, the log emission, cut before the bench), each put with a
recommendation; the owner's answers are recorded here as
`roadmap-hcd.md`'s table records its phases'.

| Decision | Taken |
|---|---|
| Generic by best effort | Owner, 2026-10-14: the Thunderbolt 3 and USB4 work is made as generic as the evidence allows; on 2026-10-15, as many controllers as possible. Record 21 section 1.4 is the answer: the host interface and re-enumeration are generic; the ICM client is generic across devices and per-generation across the Intel firmware-ICM hosts it addresses, through a hardware profile and a message and lifecycle profile per generation; real USB4 hosts are the software connection manager (Phase 40, design record 22), and an unknown firmware connection manager is unsupported |
| Phase numbers | Owner, 2026-10-15: `TB3` is Phase 39 (`39-0.n`, `39-A.n`, `39-V.n`, `39-E.n`, `39.final`), `U4CM` is Phase 40 (`40.n`, `40.final`); after 40.1, Phase 40's work is split into `40-0.n`, `40-A.n` and `40-V.n` as Phase 39's is (orchestrator, 2026-10-15, for the owner to confirm) |
| Sources | Owner, 2026-10-15: Linux `drivers/thunderbolt` and FreeBSD `sys/dev/thunderbolt` may be read as interface documentation, under the rule design record 20's reading of Linux's xHCI quirks followed (no function body copied); record 21 section 9. Widened the same day, through the `usb4-emu` maintainer's note, once `usb4-emu`'s relicensing to GPL-2.0-only landed (`3c74189`): code from `usb4-emu` (`tools/usb4cm` included), Linux and FreeBSD may be adapted for this work, notices and SPDX tags kept, a `legal-provenance.md` section 2a entry per adapted file; behaviour from Linux alone stays [linux]; nothing moves into `nvme98` (`legal-provenance.md` section 3) |
| One binary (D1) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: `xhcicm.sys` in `src\cm\` with its own INFs, allowlists and gate |
| The NT filter's install (D2) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: a separate NT-only INF adding a device-specific `UpperFilters`; answered by `39-0.4`: the device-specific route (Include/Needs `machine.inf` `PCI_DRV`), unforced on 2000, forced on XP and 7; the filter INF staged in `nt\`, out of the 9x path, `&CC_0604` lines only |
| Interrupts (D3) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: none, polling only, 200 ms idle |
| Approval policy (D4) | Owner, 2026-10-14: `XhciCmApprove` 0 and 1 only, 1 the default; nothing is remembered across reboots, the firmware and the boot ACL holding the state that matters; "approve known devices only, from a hand-filled UUID list" is a later option, not a value (record 21 section 5.4) |
| The absent controller (D5) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: nothing; a known limitation, its power-on being an ACPI method out of reach on 98 |
| The `nvme98` channel (D6) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: record 21 section 7.2's proposal, shipped as `39-A.7` once `nvme98` has chosen, the statement withheld until qualified |
| Version and cut (D7) | Owner, 2026-10-15, changed: `3.0.0.0` ships both phases; `39.final` closes Phase 39 without a cut and `40.final` cuts `3.0.0.0` |
| Names (D8) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: `xhcicm.sys`, `xhcicm.inf`, `xhcicmf.inf`, class System; the device descriptions revised by the owner the same day to name each controller's generation: `xHCI98 Thunderbolt 2 Controller`, `xHCI98 Thunderbolt 3 Controller`, `xHCI98 USB4 Host Router` (from 3.2.0.0 `xHCI98 TB2 Controller`, `xHCI98 TB3 Controller`, `xHCI98 USB4 Router` with the maker and product per id, task 43.73), and for the ports the filter attaches to `xHCI98 Thunderbolt PCI Bridge`, `xHCI98 USB4 PCI Bridge`, `xHCI98 PCI Bridge` (record 21 section 3.3) |
| The NHI ids (D9) | Owner, 2026-10-15, changed: every Alpine Ridge and Titan Ridge NHI claimed from a public id listing, never untested without a plain warning; widened the same day, with the source rule, to every Intel firmware-connection-manager host Linux addresses (Falcon Ridge, Alpine Ridge, Titan Ridge, Maple Ridge, Ice Lake to Meteor Lake), only `15BF` captured, the untested ids acting by default with the warning in the README and release notes (owner); the widening's shape - four-test selection with an inert start, hardware profiles apart from message profiles, per-domain tunnel-port sets, no reset or takeover by the ICM client - is the orchestrator's decision of 2026-10-15 after review, for the owner to confirm (record 21 sections 1.4, 3.3, 6.3) |
| Safe eject (D10) | Owner, 2026-10-15, changed: in Phase 39 as `39-A.8`; NT through the filter's answer to `IRP_MN_QUERY_CAPABILITIES`, 98 SE and ME through CONFIGMG best effort, NT-only with a release-notes line if the 9x half fails; the eject stops the drivers and the user unplugs. Two refinements, the orchestrator's for the owner to confirm: the mark goes on the children of verified tunnel ports, never on a port itself, and `SurpriseRemovalOK` is set only where `39-V.2` shows Windows still lists the device with it (record 21 section 7.4) |
| A master switch (D11) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: `XhciCmMode`, default 1 (auto), 0 off (the inert start), 2 the ICM client on an Intel id the profile table does not list, at the user's own risk and with that id added to a copy of the INF by the user, 3 the software connection manager's experimental override (design record 22); the install itself is the opt-in, so the default is on (record 21 section 8) |
| The log emission (D12) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: nothing in `release`, `#if DBG` in `debug` and `qemu` |
| Cut before the bench (D13) | Owner, 2026-10-15, as recommended: the VM legs and gates before the cut, `39-E.1` and `39-E.2` after it on the asset, a failure there a re-cut; the cut is `40.final`'s |
| Time | Owner, 2026-10-15: no task is cut to meet a time; the run goes late instead |
| Phase 42 | Owner, 2026-10-18: the planned items promoted that day are Phase 42, for `3.1.0.0`, with the ids `42-0.n` (the designs), `42-A.n` (the code), `42-V.n` (the legs) and `42.final`: INSTUI.1 is 42-0.1, DI9X.1 is 42-0.2, SNAP.1 is 42-0.3, EJRB.1 is 42-0.4 (its design) and 42-A.5 (its code), DI9X.2 is 42-A.1, INSTUI.2 is 42-A.2 (its legs 42-V.1), SNAP.2 is 42-A.4, GEN1.1 is 42-A.6, QINR.1 is 42-A.7, UNPL.1 is 42-A.8 and TB3NAME.1 is 42-A.9; SNAP.3 (the removal) and GEN1.2 (the multi-drive legs) stay in "Planned work", as do the bench and hardware readings; UNPL.1 back under its own id on 2026-10-20 ("The items left by `3.1.0.0`") |
| The uninstall | Owner, 2026-10-18: the drivers uninstall as NUSB's do, from Add/Remove Programs in one step, on every target, leaving the machine as it was before the install (42-A.3) |
| Phase 42, widened | Owner, 2026-10-18, going through every planned item one by one: into Phase 42 - SNAP.3 (with SNAP.2 as 42-A.4: the removal in `3.1.0.0`, no deprecation release between), IDLE.1 as 42-V.4, U4REC.1 as 42-0.5 and 42-A.10, NVHW.1 as 42-A.11 and 42-V.8 (Stor98's MD-2 driven from here too), NV9X.2 and NV9X.3 as 42-0.7, 42-A.12, 42-A.13 and 42-V.6, EMU.2 to EMU.5 as 42-V.5, PCI9X.1 as 42-A.14, NOIRQ.1 as 42-0.6 and 42-A.15, GEN1.2 as 42-V.7; dropped - SLIP.1 (the F6 floppy serves 2000 and XP Setup; issue 16 keeps the attempt); closed, every task done - the x64 legs owed at the cut (X64.1, X64.2), the emulator's Thunderbolt 3 dock behind a USB4 host on 2000 (EMU.1, read 2026-10-17 with 40-A.8), the sign-only packaging mode (SIGN.1) and Stor98 the renamed driver (STOR.1, STOR.2), their records in `runs/run-41.md` and `build-and-test.md`; kept in "Planned work" - B24.5 (bench), NOX.1 (rare), PCR.1 (no safe proof reachable, record 17 section 4.13), U4HW.1 and S3W2K.1 (hardware); IDLE.1, NVHW.1, NV9X.3, EMU.2 to EMU.5 and NOIRQ.1 (its code) back under their own ids on 2026-10-20 ("The items left by `3.1.0.0`") |
| The machine-wide registry key | Owner, 2026-10-18, on SweetLow's proposal (42-A.17): every `Xhci*` value of `xhci98.sys` and every `XhciCm*` value of `xhcicm.sys` is also read from `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\System\CurrentControlSet\Services\USB`; a value there wins over the controller's or host interface's driver-key value, which stays the fallback (the INFs write every per-controller value at install, so the other precedence would leave the machine-wide key dead); the helpers and the installer write the machine-wide value and show both; XHCITR shows both and which won. Not taken: the drivers' own service keys (two keys, and 9x creates none for a WDM driver), a key under `Software` (the wrong hive for a driver that may start before it), per-controller precedence with the INFs no longer writing per-controller values (too large for `3.1.0.0`) |
| `XhciFastPollFsLs` 4, the direct encoding | Owner, 2026-10-18, on SweetLow's proposal: value 4 takes the new scheme (codes 128 to 143 program Interval = code - 128) in `3.1.0.0` in place of the 36.19 codes 128 to 130, no new value and no compatibility, since nobody uses value 4 yet (42-A.18) |
| Stor98 on 98 SE and ME, a newer generation | Owner, 2026-10-18, from Stor98's audit of that day: an entry of the link-state channel at a `LinkGeneration` above the one a Stor98 instance is bound to, without `DOWN_QUIESCED`, is evidence that its own generation is quiesced only once the instance has lost presence (gone, or its controller reads all-ones); while the controller still answers it is identity loss - no evidence, nothing completed, no acknowledgement, the drive keeps running and a later presence loss ends at Stor98's gone deadline. Its pool takes only `DOWN_QUIESCED` from the table, plus a later instance's acknowledged disable of the same function. Not taken: the bump as evidence everywhere (the contract's earlier letter, which with 42-A.6's bump completes a working drive's requests while it may still DMA), and never evidence (a real re-plug would then always wait for the deadline). `stor98cm.h` now says so, and that only a new tunnel increments `LinkGeneration` (42-A.6, 42-A.19) |
| Phase 42, the open choices | Owner, 2026-10-18, the choices its tasks left open: the installer (42-0.1) is a window, a wizard that walks the user through the key options and ends by asking for the restart; its pages - welcome, what was found, the components (`xhci98.sys` and `xhciuas.sys` required, Thunderbolt 3 / USB4 support ticked as record 23 section 8.5 decides its default and with its warning, the bundled Stor98 never ticked unasked, the tools folder, `C:\XHCI98` by default), the USB settings, the Thunderbolt settings (when ticked), Stor98's settings (when ticked), the summary, the progress, and the finish with "Restart now" (the default) and "Restart later"; a settings page shows the values record 23 section 8.1 asks, each only where its "Asked when" holds, with its default and its meaning, an update keeping a value the user set; the values never asked behind an "Advanced..." button, each with its default and a warning; on 98 SE and ME a device 42-A.1's helper could not install, or whose wizard the user cancelled, gets a page with that device's Update Driver steps, which the user can save to a text file, and the install completes for the rest. The 98 SE and ME helper (42-0.2) is built with Open Watcom 2.0 against the 98 SE DDK's `SETUPX.H` and `SETUPX.LIB` (`tools/win98ddk-extracted/`). `XHCISNAP` (42-0.3): everything it does that XHCITR does not moves into XHCITR - the `.DEV` device report, the slot and enumeration regions, the raw `.BIN` and `.PSC` dump, `-probe` - the counter-name check moves to a build script, and `xhcisnap\` is deleted. The safe-removal mark's writer (42-0.4) is a privileged NT service. The uninstall (42-A.3) removes the bundled Stor98 only when this package's installer installed it, and restores nothing of another USB stack present before the install (NUSB's, say): Windows finds the controller again. The unsafe-unplug notice (42-A.8, now UNPL.1) is NT-only in `3.1.0.0`, said so in the release notes. 42-V.7 and 42-V.8 (now NVHW.2) are owed after the cut. 42-A.16 (now SRSTOP.1) was seen with a USB stick |
| The connection manager alone | Owner, 2026-10-19: "The installer should give people the option not to install xhci98 but just the CM. Reason is that in case some Win 8 users which have native USB3 but not USB4 or TB may want it" (task 42-A.21). Taken in writing it, the owner to confirm: offered on 2000 to 7 when the package carries the connection manager, the only install on Windows 8 and 8.1 and never on 98 SE and ME (no USB 3 driver of their own there); 10 and later refused as before (11 has a USB4 connection manager of its own); the bundled Stor98 not offered on 8 and 8.1; a run that leaves the USB drivers out never removes an installed `xhci98.sys`, the way back being the uninstall and a run with the connection manager alone; one Add/Remove Programs entry, named by what is registered; Windows 8 and 8.1 described in the release notes as installable for the connection manager alone and never run here, not as a target (record 23 section 4.5, D17 to D19). The owner, the same day: "let user update existing driver by installing over it. Should not have multiple entries in Add remove programs in Control Panel." - one entry, `Uninstall\xhci98`, for every set of components, an install over any of them an update in place, none under `Wow6432Node` (record 26 section 9.1) |
| The items left by `3.1.0.0` | Owner, 2026-10-20: the Phase 42 items moved to "Planned - Left by `3.1.0.0`" take planned ids, the earlier one where they had one, each keeping its 42 id as "was": 42-A.8 is UNPL.1, 42-A.11 NVHW.1, 42-A.13 NV9X.3, 42-A.15 NOIRQ.1, 42-A.16 SRSTOP.1, 42-A.34 SILENT.2, 42-A.35 S98NAME.1, 42-A.36 PRECUT.1, 42-A.37 NUSBPKG.1, 42-A.38 EJTEXT.1, 42-V.4 IDLE.1, 42-V.5 EMU.2 to EMU.5, 42-V.6 NV9X.4, 42-V.8 NVHW.2, 42-V.10 GEN1.3, 42-V.11 EMUFAST.1 and 42-V.12 OWED31.1 |
| Phase 43 | Owner, 2026-10-20, going through every planned item one by one: Phase 43, for `3.2.0.0`, with plain ids `43.n` and `43.final`, ordered by ease, local virtual-machine work first (renumbered three times that day, before any published file cited them). (holds 2 and 3); a hub's held port is said at its controller; with no hold known, a port that failed enumeration says "If this keeps failing, unplug the device and plug it in again."; the tray reads the holds every 10 s and announces a new hold once in a balloon (98 SE: the pop-up), one a watch, the option it follows and the switched-off port's text being the owner's decisions of 2026-11-14 below (record 25 section 24). 49.40, 49.41 and 49.42 are the rows above |

## Phase sequence

Phase 39 is the Thunderbolt firmware-connection-manager client, tunnel
re-enumeration and safe eject; Phase 40 the USB4 software connection
manager; Phase 41 the graphical tree, eject and tray tool XHCITR; all
three ship as `3.0.0.0`. Real USB4 devices follow the ORICO capture
and are planned task U4HW.1 (in Phase 44 from 2026-10-25: 44.39 and 44-E.6; its bench 45-E.5 from 2026-10-29). The order is the handoff's: the
Thunderbolt 3 client is provable on real hardware the owner holds; the
software connection manager is provable, for now, only against the
emulator and a Framework 13 capture.

---

## Phase 39 - The Thunderbolt 3 ICM Client, Tunnel Re-enumeration and Safe Eject

Goal: a Thunderbolt 3 dock or enclosure plugged into an Alpine Ridge
machine after boot, or with pre-boot support off, gets its PCIe tunnel and
its devices appear and disappear under Windows 98 SE and Windows 2000, and
can be safely ejected where the operating system offers it: a client of
the controller's firmware connection manager (the ICM) as a WDM driver of
the host interface, the re-enumeration of the tunnel ports' buses after
each change, and the removable mark on the devices behind them; with the
other Intel firmware-connection-manager hosts claimed on profiles read from
the Linux driver, untested and said so. The first of the three steps the
`usb4-emu` handoff orders (`usb4-emu`'s `docs\XHCI98-HANDOFF.md`
section 2, maintainer 2026-10-14): this, then the USB4 software connection
manager (Phase 40), then real USB4 devices once the ORICO enclosure capture
exists.

Status: closed 2026-10-17 on the `3.0.0.0` cut, with 39-E.1 left for after
it on the owner's bench (D13), dropped by the owner unread on 2026-10-25 and ticked by the owner as done on the owner's bench on 2026-11-01 (its task line). Opened 2026-10-15 by the owner, from the planned work `TB3`
drawn up on 2026-10-14 from the handoff, the `ICM-PROTOCOL.md` contract,
the re-enumeration experiment and the P14s captures; on branch `3.0.0.0`.
Design record 21 is the plan, its decisions D1 to D13 taken (D4 on
2026-10-14, the rest on 2026-10-15). Released with Phase 40 as `3.0.0.0`
(D7): `39.final` closes this phase and `40.final` cuts. Vehicle: the
`usb4-emu` QEMU fork's `tb3-alpine-ridge` profile through
`scripts/run-win98.sh` (`BIOS=`, `DEVICE_AT_BOOT=`) and the Alpine Ridge
tree on the Windows 2000 guest, read-only in that repository, with this
project's own overlays under `vm\usb4\` and ports 47900 to 47999 (record
21 section 10.2); the owner's P14s Gen 1 and TS3 Plus dock for the bench.
No other generation has an emulator profile or a machine here. Released on `main` as the squash commit `e26309cb` ("3.0.0.0 (#22)").

Why a phase: a second new binary (`xhcicm.sys`) with its own INFs, gates
and package rows; a new hardware contract read from captures and from the
Linux driver rather than a specification; three operating-system
mechanisms (CONFIGMG on 9x, a bridge filter and child filters on NT) no
file here has used; and a release.

Batches: `-0` static, `-A` host, `-V` the VM legs, `-E` the bench, and
`.final`. The ids were `TB3-0.n`, `TB3-A.n`, `TB3-V.n`, `TB3-E.n` and
`TB3.final` until the owner opened the phase.

## Phase 40 - The USB4 Software Connection Manager

Goal: real USB4 hosts, all vendors, where the operating system is the
connection manager: the second step of the handoff's order, after Phase
39. Contract: the USB4 Specification 2.0 and Connection Manager Guide 2.0,
`usb4-emu/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` sections 4, 5 and 7 and `WINDOWS-DEBUG.md`
("What the Windows CM required of the model"), and Linux's and FreeBSD's
drivers, whose code, like `usb4-emu`'s, may be adapted with its notices
kept since `usb4-emu`'s relicensing to GPL-2.0-only landed on 2026-10-15
(`AGENTS.md`, "Reference Implementations"; `legal-provenance.md` section
3). Start from `usb4-emu/tools/usb4cm/core` (GPL-2.0-only from `3c74189`,
C89, polling, 32-bit DMA, about 1.3 thousand lines; it brought a tunnel up
from the Windows 98 DOS prompt on 2026-10-14), in the same binary as the
ICM client, admitted by the
positive software-connection-manager rule record 21 section 1.4.2 outlines
and design record 22 defines. Test on `usb4-generic` with both host models
(`minimal`, `intel-adl`) and with `preos-cm=on` (the pre-OS tunnel cleanup
the Windows 11 CM performs). Write it to depend on no exact device router:
every router operation possibly unsupported, adapter numbers discovered
from the capability chain and the PCIe Downstream Entry Mapping operation,
every completion polled with a timeout. The handoff's size estimate is 4
to 7 thousand lines. Under a software connection manager this driver
tears paths down itself, so `nvme98`'s obligation 2 (record 21 section 7)
becomes real work here: the link-down statement only after the path is
closed and the endpoint's reset has completed.

Status: closed 2026-10-17 on the `3.0.0.0` cut (`40.final`). Opened
2026-10-15 by the owner, from the planned work
`U4CM`; released with Phase 39 as `3.0.0.0` (D7), the cut being
`40.final`'s. Its design record is design record 22. Nothing in it waits
for the ORICO USB4 enclosure capture except planned task U4HW.1 (now 44.39, its bench 45-E.5), which adds a real
device-router preset and a conformance check. Released on `main` as the squash commit `e26309cb` ("3.0.0.0 (#22)").

## Phase 41 - XHCITR, the USB and Thunderbolt Tree with Eject and Tray Icon

Goal: a graphical tool for every target that shows every USB controller,
hub and device and every Thunderbolt 3 / USB4 host, router, tunnel and
the Windows devices behind it, with their details, ejects a tunnelled
device as Windows' own Safely Remove Hardware does, and sits in the
notification area, starting at login. `INSTALL /devices` and
`INSTALL /eject` (design record 24) stay as they are.

Status: closed 2026-10-17 on the `3.0.0.0` cut. Opened 2026-10-16 by the owner, who held `3.0.0.0` for it;
it ships in `3.0.0.0` with Phases 39 and 40, and `40.final`'s cut comes
after `41.final`. Its design record is design record 25. Released on `main` as the squash commit `e26309cb` ("3.0.0.0 (#22)").

## Phase 42 - Release `3.1.0.0`: the Windowed Installer, the Uninstall, the Windows 98 SE and ME Device Install, XHCISNAP Retired, and the Connection-Manager Follow-Ups

Goal: a windowed installer in place of the `INSTALL.BAT` console tool,
installing, updating and setting every driver of the package on every
target; an uninstall from Add/Remove Programs in one step, as NUSB's;
the driver put on the controller and its root hub by the tool itself on
98 SE and ME; `XHCISNAP` retired into XHCITR; the safe-removal mark kept
across a stack rebuild on NT; the connection-manager follow-ups; released
as `3.1.0.0`.

Status: closed on the `3.1.0.0` cut of 2026-10-20, on branch `3.1.0.0`. Opened 2026-10-18 by the owner from
"Planned work", with the uninstall (42-A.3) added and the phase widened
the same day when the owner went through every planned item; the
decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks. SweetLow's first tests
of `3.0.0.0`, Stor98's audit and its move into this repository, the
bench and the matrices added 42-A.16 to 42-A.38 from 2026-10-18 to
2026-10-20. On 2026-10-19 and 2026-10-20 the owner moved what this
release does not finish to "Planned - Left by `3.1.0.0`" below, under
planned ids of their own, and reworded the legs to what was read. It
closes with the `3.1.0.0` cut. Its records are `runs/run-42.md`, design
records 26, 27 and 28, and record 21 sections 7.2 and 7.4 and record 22
section 7.7. Released on `main` as the squash commit `6b74d071` ("3.1.0.0 (#23)").

## Phase 43 - Release `3.2.0.0`: the Cut's Checks, Faster Emulator Legs, the Unsafe-Unplug Notice, the 9x Stor98 Channel, and What `3.1.0.0` Owed

Goal: the cut's text checks run without a build and the emulator legs
run faster; NUSB's 98 SE storage files in the release download from a
driver pack kept in a separate repository, offered by the installer, and
three downloads in all; the installer's and the eject's words corrected,
Stor98 named as part of the package; the unsafe-unplug notice on NT, the
"can run faster" notice and a red tray icon; the Stor98 channel's two 9x
transactions, and large requests reaching Stor98 whole on 98 SE and ME;
a refused interface's device id settled; Windows 8, 8.1 and 10 targets
of the connection manager and Stor98, with `xhci98.sys` an opt-in there;
a larger isochronous ring; the idle poll default decided; the
virtual-machine legs `3.1.0.0` left; released as `3.2.0.0`.

Status: closed on the `3.2.0.0` cut of 2026-10-24, on branch `3.2.0.0`.
Opened 2026-10-20 by the owner from "Planned work", going through every
planned item; the decisions table maps the planned ids to its tasks,
which ran in order of ease (owner, the same day). The legs, the owner
and the coordinator added 43.42 to 43.73 from 2026-10-20 to 2026-10-23;
43.41 was deferred as ISORING.4. At the cut what this release does not
finish moved to "Planned - Left by `3.2.0.0`" below, under planned ids
of its own. Its records are `runs/run-43.md` and `runs/run-43-24.md`,
design records 29 and 30, and the sections of records 13, 19, 20, 21,
22, 23, 24, 25, 26 and 27 its items name. Released on `main` as the squash commit `01bdcfa5` ("3.2.0.0 (#24)").

- [x] 43.14 a refused interface's device id on 98 SE and ME, read first (was NOX.1's reading) (done 2026-10-22: a VID/PID line binds it; `runs/run-43.md`, "43.14") NOX.1's plan, moved here from "Planned work" on 2026-10-26 (owner): a refused storage interface's device id on Windows 98 SE and ME (from the 38.2 legs, 2026-10-13; owner, the same day: read first, a task of its own). Record 13 and `src\xhci_xport.h` say a REFUSED interface (no transport it can run, task 29-A) "shows no VID/PID-derived hardware id at all", its one hardware id `USB\XHCI98_NOXPORT&VID_v&PID_p&REV_r[&MI_nn]`, so neither `usbstor.inf` nor a vendor INF can bind it. On 98 SE the refused `usb-uas` devnode's registry `HardwareID` read `"USB\\XHCI98_NOXPORT&VID_46F4&PID_0003&REV_0000,USB\\VID_46F4&PID_0003"` (runtime, `Enum\USB\VID_46F4&PID_0003\P382R2`, `out\p38-2\legs\`): the second entry is the bus's device id (`BusQueryDeviceID`), which the PDO still reports in VID/PID form. Read first, on 98 SE and ME (with NUSB 3.3's stack too, whose `usbstor.inf` names 135 VID/PID lines): whether an INF whose only line is that VID/PID binds a refused device. If it does, the refused PDO's device id takes the `XHCI98_NOXPORT` form as well (its instance path moves, so the design says what becomes of a devnode an earlier version made), checked against 38.9's move of the bus's own ids to `USB\XHCI98_...`; if it does not, record 13 and `xhci_xport.h` say why the device id is harmless there. Either way the claim is corrected to what was read, NT's matching (hardware and compatible ids only) stated as read or as not read. Host tests of the id, both builds, legs on 98 SE, ME and one NT target with a UAS-only device at SuperSpeed on a controller that does not stream

## Phase 44 - Release `3.3.0.0`: Stor98 for Tunnelled Drives Only, the Connection Manager Off the Boot Path, the Hibernate Resume, the Installer's Pages, and What `3.2.0.0` Owed

Goal: Stor98 bound only to drives the connection manager confirms behind
a tunnel; the connection manager no longer a boot driver, the
installer's test-signing gate on Vista and 7 x64 and its Secure Boot and
Intel Thunderbolt readings; a tunnel this driver built rebuilt after a
hibernate; the ASMedia ASM4242 host, its
prefetchable windows moved below 4 GB on 32-bit systems by `XHCIPFW`,
and 2000's interrupt for a hot-added drive; the installer's Other USB
drivers page split, its Welcome page taller and 98 SE's and ME's audio
packages found again; the USB Tree's generation line, version warning
and PCI diagnostics; the cut's text checks before any build, an
installer-only rebuild, window legs driven headless and the cut's two
architectures built at once; the project's source relicensed
GPL-2.0-or-later; XHCICLN dropped; the emulator legs `3.1.0.0` left and
the virtual-machine legs `3.2.0.0` left; the free driver pack at
`usb-wdm-free` 0.1.4 and the non-free pack's Ethernet drivers; released
as `3.3.0.0`, with the bench after the cut.

Status: closed on the `3.3.0.0` cut of 2026-10-28, on branch `3.3.0.0`.
Opened 2026-10-25 by the owner from "Planned work", going through every
planned item; the decisions table ("Phase 44") maps the planned ids to
its tasks and records what was dropped, closed or kept. The tasks ran in
order of ease, STORONLY.1 first after the text checks (owner,
2026-10-25); the owner and the coordinator added 44.35 to 44.82 from
2026-10-25 to 2026-10-28. At the cut what this release does not finish
moved to "Planned work": 44.69 as INSTREC.1 (owner, 2026-10-28; its
task line left with it, and the id is not reused); 44.36's speedups as
NVPERF.1, 44.72's option (c) as PFW8.1, and the dock and chain code of
44.66 and 44.67 as DOCK.1 and CHAIN.1 to CHAIN.5. On 2026-10-29 the
owner took them into Phase 45 (45.5, 45.9, 45.10, 45.12 to 45.18 and
45-E.9) and removed the emptied sections; PFW8.1's code and NVPERF.1's
changes stay under "Planned work". The install legs,
44-E.7, are read after the cut on the published asset (as decision
D13), a failure there a re-cut; the real-hardware bench, 44-E.1 to
44-E.6, moved to "Planned work" (owner, 2026-10-28) and into Phase 45
as 45-E.1 to 45-E.5 and 45-E.8 on 2026-10-29. Its records are `runs/run-44.md`,
design records 31, 32, 33 and 34, issues 19 and 20, Stor98's PLAN, and
the sections of records 13, 17, 20, 21, 22, 23, 25, 26, 28, 29 and 30
its items name. Released on `main` as the squash commit `c40b7ae4` ("3.3.0.0 (#27)").

## Phase 45 - Release `3.4.0.0`: Hot-Added Docks and Daisy Chains, the Kept Child, and What `3.3.0.0` Left

Goal: a dock plugged in while 2000 and XP run (design record 33), and a
chain of a device behind a device on NT under the firmware and the
software connection managers (design record 34), with Stor98's
statements for chained drives; the kept kernel child prototyped; USB4
version 2 links and their Gen 4 link recovery; Thunderbolt networking
between two hosts (design record 35); `XHCIPFW.SYS` as a root-bus filter
on the 32-bit 8 and 8.1; one release build for everyone and the download
laid out by Windows family (design record 36); a faster cut build and
self-tests skipped when unchanged; the installer's Thunderbolt and USB4
page, its full Advanced lists, its Help and every registry value written
at its default; 98 SE's and ME's staging, page and storage record; the
free and non-free `rtl815x` side by side; the USB Tree's version in its
title; the users' reports on `3.3.0.0` read and fixed where they are
this package's; a Driver Verifier pass on 10 x64; released as `3.4.0.0`,
with the bench after the cut.

Status: closed on the `3.4.0.0` cut of 2026-11-01, on branch `3.4.0.0`
(the release commit `75b3db38`; released on `main` as the squash commit `6960856d`, "3.4.0.0 (#29)"). Opened 2026-10-29 by
the owner from "Planned work", going through every planned item one by
one; the decisions table ("Phase 45" and "Phase 45 additions") maps the
planned ids to its tasks and records what was kept. The tasks ran in
order of ease, STORCDDB.1 first (owner, 2026-10-29), the chains in the
owner's order of 2026-10-27; 45.20 to 45.66 were added from 2026-10-29
to 2026-10-31 by the owner, from the users' reports and from what the
legs found. At the cut, 2026-11-01, the tasks deferred to the next
release (45.11, 45.14, 45.19, 45.21, 45.22, 45.25 and 45.26) and the
real-hardware bench, 45-E.1 to 45-E.15, moved to "Planned - Left by
`3.4.0.0`" with their ids (owner, 2026-11-01); 45.1 and 45.57 were
dropped. The owner ticked 45.15, 45.17, 45.27, 45.39, 45.40, 45.41 and
45.56 on 2026-11-01 with their hardware (for 45.40 the user's) reading
owed, and 45.59 after its x64 leg on the signed release, the bench owed.
The install legs were read on the cut's downloads (45.final), one 98 SE
shut-down hang after the update not reproduced and released by the owner.
Its records are `runs/run-45.md`, design records 33, 34, 35 and 36, and
the sections of records 08, 13, 17, 20, 21, 22, 23, 26, 27, 28, 30, 31
and 32 its items name.

- [-] 45.1 the update from `3.2.0.0` on 2000 and XP removing the class key `CriticalDeviceDatabase\pci#cc_010802` that binds Stor98, and its uninstall twin (was STORCDDB.1; found by 44-E.7's after-cut leg on 2000, 2026-10-29) (dropped by the owner, 2026-10-29: only a 2000 or XP `3.2.0.0` install with its Thunderbolt box ticked carries it, and the drive still works; the manual deletion a known limitation, 45.final)

## Phase 46 - Release `3.4.1.0`: Stor98 Only on Its Own Drive and Its Interrupt Watchdog, the 98 SE and ME USB4 Host Found by the Installer, and the Installer's and USB Tree's Fixes

Goal: Stor98 on 98 SE and ME bringing up only the drive it was loaded
for, never Windows' own NVMe system disk, read in the emulator and on
the owner's USB4 board first of all; the installer finding a USB4 host
on 98 SE and ME whose router Windows has not listed; Stor98's INF
comments and its missed-interrupt watchdog; a held shared IRQ named at
start; the installer's settings split by page, its progress bar, its
texts in the owner's words and its Thunderbolt box the user's; XHCITR's
Save Snapshot without its window, and the firmware tables saved; AMD's
USB4 hosts named; the ASM4242 handover on 2000 and XP; the users'
reports on `3.4.0.0` read and fixed where they are this package's; the
documentation under CC BY 4.0; released as the quick `3.4.1.0`, with
the bench after the cut.

Status: closed on the `3.4.1.0` cut of 2026-11-05 (the signed cut made just after midnight, 2026-11-06; released on `main` as the squash commit `3bafc050`, "3.4.1.0 (#30)").
Opened 2026-11-01 by the owner for `3.5.0.0`, going through every
planned item one by one, and made the quick `3.4.1.0` the same day (the
decisions table, "Phase 46" and "`3.4.1.0`"): 46.2 dropped (the fix
ships instead, and the release notes and history say what `3.4.0.0`
did), the first list's 46.9 to 46.14 back to their Phase 45 ids, their
numbers used again. 46.1 first, with 46-E.1 on the owner's board as
soon as it had a build (owner, 2026-11-01: the highest priority), the
rest in order of ease; 46.11 (the decisions table, "46.11") and the
tasks from 46.13 on were added from 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-05 by the
owner, from the bench, the users' reports on `3.4.0.0` and what the
legs found. The owner's review of the open tasks, 2026-11-05, ticked
46.1, 46.6, 46.23 and 46-E.1, ticked and dropped 46.13, 46.14, 46.16
and 46.20 (reopened only with a fresh report), and moved 46.19's
symptom (a) to Planned as CMDTO.1; at the cut, 2026-11-05, 46.11 and
46.12 moved to "Planned - Left by `3.4.1.0`" with their ids, beside
S98FWD.1, FWTAKE.1 and USBCOMPAT.1, and 46.25 was ticked with its
bench owed. 46.17, 46.30 and 46.final close at the cut. The real-hardware
bench 45-E.1 to 45-E.15 stays in "Planned - Left by `3.4.0.0`". Its
records are `runs/run-46.md`, issues 21 to 24, Stor98's PLAN, and the
sections of design records 13, 15, 18, 20, 21, 22, 23, 25, 26, 27, 28,
32 and 36 its items name.

## Phase 47 - Release `3.4.2.0`: the Audit Fixes of 2026-11-06 and the `3.4.x` Follow-Ups

Goal: the fixes of the whole-repository audit of 2026-11-06 and the
`3.4.x` follow-ups the owner queued after the `3.4.1.0` cut; the planned
items the owner took in the same day; the users' reports of the phase
(the 9x USB audio unplug, a UAS drive's SMART reads and its Code 10, the
HP EliteBooks on 2000 and XP); XHCITR's Settings window and quick save;
the pins of `usb-wdm-free` `v0.1.9` and `usb-wdm-nonfree` `v1.3.0`;
released as `3.4.2.0`.

Status: closed on the `3.4.2.0` cut of 2026-11-08, on branch `3.4.2.0`
(the cut signed by the owner on 2026-11-08, re-cut that evening with
the readme fixes `c6919297` and `62633d90`; released on `main` as the
squash commit `f30a8f32`, "3.4.2.0 (#31)"). Opened 2026-11-06 by the
owner from the tip of the `3.4.1.0` cut (the decisions table, "Phase
47"): the audit's fixes as 47.1, the queued follow-ups as 47.2 to 47.6,
and 47.7 to 47.21 from "Planned work" the same day; 47.22 to 47.45 were
added from 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-08 by the owner, from the audit, the
users' reports (issues 25 to 27) and what the legs found. On 2026-11-07
the tasks that were `usb-wdm-free`'s own left the phase, tracked there
(owner), and on 2026-11-08 47.18, 47.19 and 47.27 closed here by that
rule, 47.12's rest became BIOSUSB.1 and 47.33's MPS HALs HPMPS.1. The
tasks ticked here are done or closed for `3.4.2.0`; the post-cut legs
of 2026-11-08 settled some of their readings, and what they leave is in
"Planned - Left by `3.4.2.0`" (owner, 2026-11-08). Its records are `runs/run-47.md`, issues 25 to 27, Stor98's PLAN,
and the sections of design records 13, 18, 22, 25, 26, 30, 32, 35 and 37
its items name.

## Phase 49 - Release `3.5.0.0`: Message-Signalled Interrupts Before Vista, Thunderbolt Standby, and the `3.4.3.0` Leftovers

Goal: message-signalled interrupts for the controller on 2000 and XP
(and on 98 SE and ME if that is ready), standby and hibernation
with a Thunderbolt or USB4 drive on the computers `3.4.3.0` left out,
and the rest of what `3.4.3.0` left; released as `3.5.0.0`.

Status: closed on the `3.5.0.0` cut of 2026-11-14; opened 2026-11-10
by the owner, on branch `3.5.0.0`, from
the `3.4.3.0` release, going through every planned item (the decisions
table, "Phase 49"). The tasks done wholly in local virtual machines
come first (49.1 to 49.12), easiest first; those that need some of
the owner's bench follow (49.13 to 49.24), MSI last (owner,
2026-11-10 and 2026-11-11); 49.25 and 49.26 are faults the phase
itself found, taken in on 2026-11-11 (the decisions table, "Phase 49,
the owner's answers of 2026-11-11"), and 49.27 follows the MSI work
(owner, 2026-11-11). 49.28 to 49.39 were taken in as the phase
went (each row says by whom and when); what the phase leaves for
the next is "Planned - Left by `3.5.0.0`". On 2026-11-14 the owner
dropped the unread legs of 49.13 and 49.18, moved 49.21 to the plan,
closed 49.22 and 49.45 with their rest planned, and removed 49.23's
row (the decisions table, "Phase 49, what the unread legs become").

cut's downloads, and the phase with it (the decisions table, "Phase 49
close").

# Planned work

Not phases yet: each takes the next free phase number when the owner opens
it, and its placeholder ids take that number then (owner, 2026-10-06; the
decisions table).

## Planned - Left by `3.5.0.0`

Goal: the candidates Phase 49's work found for the next phase.

Status: not opened, unnumbered and unversioned; gathered on 2026-11-13 from
Phase 49's tasks, each from the row named (PREBOOTEJECT.1 from the
owner's decision of the same day, RIRQ9X.1 moved from 49.27 by it,
TRPORTHANG.2 moved from 49.19 by the bench decisions of the same day,
VISTASTAGE.1 moved from 49.16 by the owner's close of it,
TBSTBYWB9X.1 and TBSTBYRETRY9X.1 from the owner's answers to 49.30's
questions); INFGATEPUB.1 and WAKEPWRREL.1 added on 2026-11-14 and
planned by the owner the same day; TRSETWARN.1 added and fixed on
2026-11-14; HOLDNOTES.1 and TAKEOVER2K.1 added on 2026-11-14 and
planned by the owner the same day, when 49.43 and 49.44 were closed;
UAC2REAL.1 added and planned by the owner late on 2026-11-14;
PRECUTREL.1 added and planned by the owner after the cut, 2026-11-14;
W10TCGCHECK.1 added and planned by the owner the same evening;
TBNETCREDIT.1, MSISTAB.1 and UASLATER.1 moved from 49.21, 49.22 and
49.45 by the owner on 2026-11-14, when UASBOTAFTER.1, AMD0810.1 and
VISTASAVE.1 were dropped (the decisions table, "Phase 49, what the
unread legs become").
The owner takes each in, changes it or drops it when the next phase
opens.

- [ ] EVTSEG.1 a multi-segment event ring: several 256-entry segments, so that a controller keeps the segment every controller takes and a High Speed audio or video stream still gets the larger ring's headroom; the segment table, the dequeue's move between segments and the snapshot's decoders change with it (from 49.35; owner, 2026-11-12: "the multi-segment ring that Linux uses is left for later")
- [ ] STREAMSOFFHOLD.1 a time-out for the hold `XhciStreamsOff` puts on a SuperSpeed port: a UAS-only drive sent back to USB 2.0 that never appears there stays invisible until it is replugged or the controller is reset; after a bound, end the hold and refuse the drive in place, with a note (from 49.38's legs on 2000, read in the code under 49.39)
- [ ] MEBOOTDROP.1 98 SE and ME: a controller contained during the start drops a child ME holds stopped for its start-up rebalance, and ME never sends its removal and does not finish starting (49.16's legs, `XhciDmaStaging` 1, 2 of 2): keep the dropped child in the relations while it is stopped, refuse its next start and ask for the relations again; 9x only, legs on ME and 98 SE (from 49.16; record 17 section 4.13.9; the design's limits in `runs/run-49.md`, "49.16 (the remaining legs)")
- [ ] M4REASON.1 a failed `XhciInterruptMode` 4 start writes its reason under the controller's driver key and `XHCITR /dump` prints it, so a tester can read why the controller sits at Code 10; today only the MSI code's own refusals are readable (from 49.24's XP legs)
- [ ] RIRQBRAKE.1 the host's ring interrupt by message on 98 SE and ME (RIRQ9X.1): under light traffic a storm brake that stopped the vector goes unnoticed, the watchdog's poll keeping the ring served, and the USB Tree still says "on, by message"; look at the braked state at each maintenance pass, fall back to the poll and say so (from 49.27)
- [ ] RIRQ9X.1 the Thunderbolt and USB4 host's ring interrupt by message on 98 SE and ME, `XhciCmRingInterrupt` `2` there: 98 SE stopped during start-up with it on, on the owner's board; a missing message is not logged under light traffic; standby unread; its code left the tree on 2026-11-13 with the 98 SE and ME message path, so it starts from the history, as does mode 4 on 98 SE and ME (owner, 2026-11-13: moved from 49.27, "Close NT, move rest"; `runs/run-49.md`, "49.27")
- [ ] TUNBINDCARRY.1 a tunnel binding learned under the USB4 connection manager (48.43, 49.18), seen lost on an earlier build when Windows restarted the controller's instance before the eject, the eject then keeping the connection up with its notice; not reproduced on this release's binaries (10 x86, 2026-11-13); carry the binding across the restart with record 22 section 9.4's record of an own preserved tunnel (from 49.18)
- [x] STAGEADDR.1 dropped by the owner, 2026-11-13, with 49.15: release-build counters for 49.15's buffer addresses, which (`stage.direct.buffer`, `.mdl` and `.low`) exist in the qemu flavour alone and print to port 0xE9 alone, then 49.15's bench reading (from 49.15)
- [ ] STORFINDBOOT.1 a note for the Stor98 project, which this repository does not change: its `StorFind` reports carry no boot stamp and `PlatFindReport` ignores a failed registry write, so a stale "taken" report cannot be told from a current one; a per-boot stamp would let the 98 SE and ME eject guard (49.38) refuse a stop it cannot verify (record 24 section 7.6)
- [ ] TBSLEEPWRITE.1 49.13's S.2 case, the first task of the next release (owner, 2026-11-13): on Windows 7 to 10 with a native PCIe hot-plug tunnel port and no firmware connection manager, a drive plugged in after the start is disconnected by a standby or hibernation and must be plugged in again, the write in flight lost (`3.4.3.0` the same). The shutdown hang behind that write is fixed in `3.5.0.0` by 49.46 (`XhciCmWakePowerUp`); the designs rejected and what to test: `runs/run-49.md`, "49.13 (S.2: a write in flight across a sleep)" (from 49.13)
- [ ] PREBOOTEJECT.1 98 SE and ME: a Thunderbolt or USB4 drive connected before Windows started cannot be stopped from the USB Tree or by `INSTALL /console /eject` (as in `3.4.3.0`; `3.5.0.0` rewords the refusal and documents it); the fix is two projects' work: the connection manager exposes the Stor98 channel for a tunnel found up at the start, and Stor98 binds to it after its own start (owner, 2026-11-13: the decisions table, "Phase 49, a 98 SE or ME drive connected before Windows started")
- [ ] TRPORTHANG.2 XHCITR on 98 SE stalls for a while, then answers again, after the Thunderbolt networking node and then the port node "Port 1 (bridge 04:01:0): active, no device" are clicked on the owner's ThinkPad P14s Gen 1, an NVMe device attached earlier; seen at the `3.4.3.0` cut; read it when it happens again with the driver's log and XHCITR's trace: which request takes that long, and whether the earlier device matters (owner, 2026-11-13: moved from 49.19)
- [ ] VISTASTAGE.1 Windows Vista x86 at `XhciDmaStaging` 1 (not the default): after a controller stops answering with audio and copies running, Windows' removal stops after one or two devices, the audio's isochronous requests stay held and a forced shutdown does not finish (2 of 2, 2026-11-13; 2000 and 7 pass); a fix would release held isochronous requests earlier on a contained controller, which record 13 section 18.5 does not allow today: the owner's design decision (owner, 2026-11-13: moved from 49.16)
- [ ] TBSTBYWB9X.1 98 SE and ME standby: put a dock's or switched enclosure's own switch headers back after a wake (49.30: today the tunnel port and the function right behind it), the removal and finding again of record 21 section 6.1.5 underneath for any doubt, a chain after it; the design first; owed with it, 98 SE and ME hibernation with a tunnelled drive (49.12) (owner, 2026-11-13: from 49.30's open questions)
- [ ] TBSTBYRETRY9X.1 98 SE and ME: a removal Windows refused after a wake (a file held open, a program polling the drive) leaves the drive away (49.30); after the 20 s ask again about once a minute, never forcing a refusal (owner, 2026-11-13: from 49.30's open questions; the 2 s wait after a wake stays)
- [ ] INFGATEPUB.1 the INF gate's default and required-row checks made version-aware, so the upload assembly can re-gate a published release's INFs (the published `3.4.3.0` INFs fail on `XhciEventRingTrbs` 1024 and a missing `XhciStreamDequeueRepair`): a default by version for each row, with self-tests over the published INFs (2026-11-14; owner, 2026-11-14: planned)
- [x] TRSETWARN.1 fixed in `3.5.0.0` (owner, 2026-11-14): `XHCITR /set XhciIntelPortSwitch=1` on a computer without a listed controller printed the switch's restart warning before it refused the change (seen in 49.47's legs, 2026-11-14). `/set` now checks every change before it prints any value's note, so a refused change prints its refusal alone (the Windows 2000 STOP 0xA5 text likewise) and an accepted one prints as before; the Settings window cannot stage the value there and Setup prints no such warning first; `test_trset`'s `test_set_note`, 2026-11-14
- [ ] WAKEPWRREL.1 in place of asking for D0 after the wake (49.46), answer `PowerRelations` for a tunnelled drive's function on Windows 7 and later so the power manager orders it after the Thunderbolt host, and read whether ordinary disk I/O through the class stack makes the unit's owner power it up; neither built nor read (2026-11-14; owner, 2026-11-14: planned)
- [ ] HOLDNOTES.1 49.43's leftovers (owner, 2026-11-14, when 49.43 was closed): the USB Tree's fallback note for a port that failed enumeration, built and host-tested but not made in the emulator (never at a hub's port, record 25 section 24 item 4); the "switched off" notice on 7 x64, not seen in three tries, and the "on hold" notice on 10 x64, unclear (`runs/run-49.md`, "The x64 check of the build signed on 2026-11-14"); with logging level 0 a held port gives no tag, note or notice, so the USB Tree should learn of a hold with logging off; and a balloon Windows accepted but hid behind one of its own is not sent again
- [ ] TAKEOVER2K.1 49.44's leftovers (owner, 2026-11-14, when 49.44 was closed): the takeover and the give-back not read on Vista x64, 8 and 8.1, nor on 7 x64 on an owner-signed build (read on a test-signed build of `a30ddf03e`); the refusal with the Windows disk behind the controller read on 10 x86 alone, in the console alone and with one controller; the warning as fixed after those legs (record 23 revisions 63 and 64) not read in a guest (`runs/run-49.md`, "49.44")
- [ ] UAC2REAL.1 sound on xHCI98 from real USB Audio devices: two USB Audio dongles (`06cb:1595` and `3302:3366`) passed one at a time into a guest, xHCI98 with `uac2` of usb-wdm-free 0.1.10, a tone played and its isochronous stream read on the bus; prepared and rehearsed with the emulator's own audio device on 2026-11-14, not run with the real devices; to run alone, with nothing else running on the host (owner, 2026-11-14: moved to the plan, not run for `3.5.0.0`)
- [ ] PRECUTREL.1 the pre-cut builds the release flavour alone (owner, 2026-11-14, after the `3.5.0.0` pre-cut: "just release build will do"): `pre-cut.ps1` and `cut-builds.ps1` run `build-driver.cmd all`, which also builds the qemu flavour a cut never publishes; a switch, or the default, for `release` alone, once it is checked that nothing the cut or `make-package.ps1 -SignOnly` reads needs the qemu record; `pre-cut.ps1`'s self-test holds the `all` arguments and changes with it
- [ ] W10TCGCHECK.1 the release matrix drops the TCG cross-check of the two Windows 10 legs (owner, 2026-11-14, during `3.5.0.0`'s install legs: "can remove those cross check for Win 10 TCG"): the legs run under WHPX (49.4), and the controller's remove step under WHPX is "recorded only", a TCG reading with `-ForceTcg` on the same package being asked for; the WHPX reading to count by itself, the note and what `selftest.ps1` holds of it changed with it, and `-ForceTcg` kept or retired as the design decides. For `3.5.0.0` the cross-check was started and stopped by the owner's word, the remove step read under WHPX alone
- [ ] TBNETCREDIT.1 Thunderbolt networking's end-to-end flow control waits up to 100 ms a frame on a quiet link (Linux on the same computer 0.9 to 2.8 ms), holding 98 SE's sending to about 50 Mbit/s, and which end's credits are late is not known (`XhciCmNetE2E` stays 1); a bench round pinging from both ends with a capture on the Windows 11 end, `XhciCmNetRxFrames` 256 and 512, the ThinkPad P14s Gen 1 as the old-Windows end (`runs/run-48.md`, "Bench round 9") (was 49.21, was E2EIDLE.1; owner, 2026-11-14: moved to the plan)
- [ ] MSISTAB.1 the MSI stability improvements' readings left by 49.22: XP x64 and a standby, on a real computer; a bench item (was 49.22; owner, 2026-11-14: moved to the plan)
- [ ] UASLATER.1 the cause of 49.45: why a later Windows' UAS class driver backported to Vista and 7 puts no disk on a drive under `xhci98.sys`; it waits for a log from the field or the owner's bench kit (was 49.45; owner, 2026-11-14)

## Planned - Left by `3.4.1.0`

Goal: what Phase 46 leaves after the `3.4.1.0` cut.

Status: not opened, unnumbered and unversioned. CMDTO.1, from a user's
report on `3.4.0.0` (moved here from "Left by `3.4.0.0`" on 2026-11-06),
and USERREAD.1, the readings Phase 46's ticked tasks left for their
reporters (2026-11-06). Everything else this section held went into
Phases 47 and 48 or was dropped (the decisions table, "Phase 47" and
"Phase 48").

- [ ] CMDTO.1 a SuperSpeed UAS drive behind a VIA VL812 hub on a Fresco Logic FL1100 not listed after a cold start until it is replugged, the hub's USB 2.0 side unaffected (a user's report on `3.4.0.0`, XP x64; was 46.19 symptom (a)): two commands gave no completion within 5 s during the start's enumeration, each answered by a reset of the whole controller. Owed first: the user's snapshot on `3.4.1.0` right after a cold start with the drive missing, before the replug, whose `cmd.timeout.trb` or `cmd.givenup` note names the command; then a design. Not taken for `3.4.1.0` (owner, 2026-11-05, on a reviewed reading of a design): keeping the controller after an aborted command needs each command type's own cleanup (an Enable Slot's unknown slot, an Address Device's changed address, a Configure Endpoint's partial contexts), and the engine cannot yet tell a successful abort from a lost command
- [ ] USERREAD.1 the reporters' own readings ticked tasks left, gathered here on 2026-11-06: 45.40's (a device's identity kept across a re-enumeration), and 46.27's and 46.31's on `3.4.1.0` (the babble on a UAS stream ring, issue 22; the Invalid Stream ID errors on the FL1100, issue 24)
